Homotopy coherent companionships and conjunctions
Jaco Ruit

Abstract
. We demonstrate that companionships and conjunctions in double ∞ \infty -categories — and more generally, in double Segal spaces — extend to functors out of the free-living companionship and conjunction respectively. Specifically, we prove that these extensions are (homotopically) unique: the corresponding spaces of extensions are contractible under suitable completeness assumptions. The developed theory is then put to use to give a characterization of companions and conjoints in functor double Segal spaces in terms of so-called companionable and conjointable 2-cells. We end with an application of our results to ( ∞ , 2 ) (\infty,2) -category theory.



1.
Introduction

Double
\infty
-categories are two-dimensional
\infty
-categorical structures with objects, two directions of 1-cells: a horizontal and vertical direction, and a notion of 2-cells.
They are the weak
\infty
-categorical generalizations of Ehresmann’s strict double categories
[
Ehr63
]
, and were first introduced in the Ph.D. thesis of Haugseng
[
Hau13
]
.
It is shown by Moser
[
Mos20
]
that the strict double categories embed fully faithfully into double
\infty
-categories.
In a precise sense, double
\infty
-categories can be viewed as a generalization of
(\infty,2)
-categories,
which admit now two distinct directions for arrows. There are many examples of double
\infty
-categories. For instance,
in
[
Hau18
]
, Haugseng constructs
the double
\infty
-categories of spans. In the realm of higher algebra, there are double
\infty
-categories of algebras, maps of algebras and bimodules
[
Lur17
, Section 4.4]
.
In
[
Rui23
]
, we discuss the construction of the double
\infty
-category
of
\infty
-categories, functors and profunctors, as well as an internal variant. More generally, Haugseng constructs a double
\infty
-category
of suitably enriched
\infty
-categories in
[
Hau16
]
.

1.1.
Double Segal spaces and the cartesian closed structure

Throughout this article, we will view double
\infty
-categories as special kinds of
double Segal spaces
.
From this point of view, double
\infty
-categories are double Segal spaces with an extra
completeness
or
univalence
condition.
A self-contained and detailed introduction to double Segal spaces, alongside with the different variations
on completeness, is found in
3
.

In particular, we will demonstrate here that the ambient
\infty
-category

\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S})

of double Segal spaces is cartesian closed. To do so, we will use the language of
exponential ideals
, to be recollected in
2
,
and verify that various localizations of double Segal spaces are exponential ideals in the
\infty
-category
\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})
of presheaves on
\Delta^{\times 2}
.
If
\mathscr{P}
and
\mathscr{Q}
are double Segal spaces, then the associated internal hom in
\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S})
of functors between
\mathscr{P}
and

\mathscr{Q}
is denoted by

\mathbb{F}\mathrm{un}(\mathscr{P},\mathscr{Q}).

This double Segal space of functors may
be computed as the internal hom in
\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})
. The vertical and horizontal arrows
of
\mathbb{F}\mathrm{un}(\mathscr{P},\mathscr{Q})
are called
vertical natural transformations
and
horizontal natural transformations
.
A horizontal natural transformation
\alpha:h\rightarrow k
between two
functors
h,k:\mathscr{P}\rightarrow\mathscr{Q}
is compromised of the following data:

•

for every object
x\in\mathscr{P}
, a horizontal arrow in
\mathscr{Q}

\alpha_{x}:h(x)\rightarrow k(x),

•

for every vertical arrow
f:x\rightarrow y
in
\mathscr{P}
, a naturality 2-cell in
\mathscr{Q}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h(x)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k(x)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h(y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k(y),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{y}}

and the data of (usually, infinitely) many coherences. This is a double
\infty
-categorical variant
on the notions of vertical and horizontal natural transformations that were studied by Grandis and Paré
[
GP99
]
for strict double categories.

1.2.
Companionships and conjunctions

The main result of this paper concerns
companionships
and
conjunctions
in double Segal spaces.
These are double categorical analogs of the adjunctions
for 2-categories, and were introduced by Grandis–Paré
[
GP04
]
in the strict context.
Likewise, these analogs play an important
role in the theory and applications of double categories
[
Shu08
]

[
DPP10
]

[
Vas19
]
.
The notions of companions and conjoints may be readily generalized to the weak
\infty
-categorical setting.

In this higher setting, companions and conjoints were first considered (albeit under a different name) by Gaitsgory and Rozenblyum to set up the
(\infty,2)
-categorical theory for
their six-functor formalisms in derived algebraic geometry
[
GR17
]
.
Moreover, companions and conjoints play a central role in a double
\infty
-categorical approach to
formal category theory

[
Rui23
]
. In this approach,
companionships and conjunctions witness (co)representability of
abstract profunctors
, i.e. abstract families of presheaves.

A companionship between a vertical arrow
f:x\rightarrow y
and a horizontal arrow
F:x\rightarrow y

in a double Segal space
\mathscr{P}
is witnessed by the following data:

(1)

a
companionship unit
2-cell

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

(3)

an equivalence in the space of 2-cells of
\mathscr{P}
between the vertical identity 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

and the vertical pasting of
\eta
and
\epsilon
,

(4)

an equivalence in the space of 2-cells of
\mathscr{P}
between the horizontal identity 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces y,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

and the horizontal pasting of
\eta
and
\epsilon
.

In this context,
F
is called the companion of
f
. The equivalences of (3) and (4) witness the
triangle identities
for the companionship. The terminology
is chosen to reflect the similarity with adjunctions.
The definition of conjunctions is (formally) dual to the definition of companionships, and will be given in
4
.
We will also consider some examples of companionships and conjunctions in this section.

It is a celebrated result of Riehl and Verity
[
RV16
]
that particular choices of adjunction data in an
(\infty,2)
-category
uniquely (in the homotopic sense) upgrade to a homotopy coherent adjunction, i.e. a functor out of the
free-living adjunction
of Schanuel–Street
[
SS86
]
.
One of the central objectives of this paper is to prove an analogous result for companionships and conjunctions.

We will show that, likewise, every companionship in
\mathscr{P}
can uniquely be upgraded to a
homotopy coherent
companionship.
Analogously, a homotopy coherent companionship is another name for a functor
from the
free-living companionship
double Segal space

\mathfrak{comp}

to
\mathscr{P}
. The double Segal space
\mathfrak{comp}
is level-wise discrete and described as follows.
The space
\mathfrak{comp}_{n,m}
of
n\times m
grids of 2-cells in
\mathfrak{comp}
is given by the
set

{\mathrm{Poset}}([n]\times[m],[1])

of maps of posets
[n]\times[m]\rightarrow[1]
.
We will show the following results:

Theorem A
.

Any companionship in a double Segal space
\mathscr{P}
extends to a homotopy coherent companionship
\mathfrak{comp}\rightarrow\mathscr{P}
. Moreover,
suppose that
\eta
is a companionship unit. Then the space of
functors
\mathfrak{comp}\rightarrow\mathscr{P}
extending
\eta
is contractible.

Theorem B
.

Suppose that
f
is a vertical arrow in a locally complete double Segal space
\mathscr{P}
.
If
f
has a companion, then the space of functors
\mathfrak{comp}\rightarrow\mathscr{P}
extending
f
is contractible.

The precise statements appear as
4.12
and
4.13
.
Note that the above results justify that the double Segal space
\mathfrak{comp}
carries the name of the free-living companionship.
We will explain in
4
that one can use the above results to formally obtain dual results for conjunctions.
In this case,
the double Segal space
\mathfrak{comp}
is replaced by its appropriate dual, the
free-living conjunction
, denoted by

\mathfrak{conj}.

We will elucidate the relation of our results to the work of Riehl–Verity
[
RV16
]
in
4.19
.

1.3.
Companions of vertical natural transformations

We will use the results of
4
in
5

to study companions (and dually, conjoints) in functor double Segal spaces.
The key step is the introduction of the
notion of
companionable
and
conjointable
2-cells in a double Segal space. In a precise sense,
this is a double
\infty
-categorical variant on the
(\infty,2)
-categorical notion of
adjointability

[
Lur09a
, Definition 7.3.1.2]
for lax commutative squares. We will explain this in
5.2
. The
main result of
5
is the following:

Theorem C
.

Let
\mathscr{P}
and
\mathscr{Q}
be double Segal spaces, so that
\mathscr{Q}
is locally complete. Suppose that
\alpha:h\rightarrow k
is a horizontal natural transformation between functors
h,k:\mathscr{P}\rightarrow\mathscr{Q}
.
Then the following assertions are equivalent:

(1)

\alpha
is a companion in
\mathbb{F}\mathrm{un}(\mathscr{P},\mathscr{Q})
,

(2)

for every vertical arrow
f:x\rightarrow y
in
\mathscr{P}
, the associated naturality 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h(x)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k(x)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h(y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k(y)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{y}}

is companionable in
\mathscr{Q}
.

The precise statement appears as
5.4
in the paper.

1.4.
An application to
(\infty,2)
-category theory

As evident in the
work of Gaitsgory–Rozenblyum
[
GR17
]
, having good foundations of double
\infty
-categories
can be fruitful in building the theory of
(\infty,2)
-categories. We will attest to this principle by giving the following
application of
C
to
(\infty,2)
-category theory.

Firstly, we will show in
6
that, given
(\infty,2)
-categories
\mathscr{X}
and
\mathscr{Y}
, one can
construct an associated double
\infty
-category

\mathbb{F}\mathrm{un}^{\mathrm{lax}}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y}),

as a certain functor double
\infty
-category, such that:

•

its objects are functors
\mathscr{X}\rightarrow\mathscr{Y}
,

•

its vertical arrows are natural transformations,

•

its horizontal arrows are
lax
natural transformations.

Here, the lax natural transformations are defined using a double categorical
construction of the Gray tensor product that is due to Gaitsgory–Rozenblyum.
The horizontal fragment is given by the
(\infty,2)
-category
\mathrm{FUN}^{\mathrm{lax}}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y})

of functors and lax natural transformations.
We will show that
C
can be applied to give a quick, double categorical proof of the following theorem of Haugseng
[
Hau21
]
:

Theorem D
.

Let
\beta:k\rightarrow h
be a lax natural transformation between functors
k,h:\mathscr{X}\rightarrow\mathscr{Y}
. Then the
following assertions are equivalent:

(1)

\beta
is a right adjoint in
\mathrm{FUN}^{\mathrm{lax}}(\mathscr{X},\mathscr{Y})
,

(2)

for any arrow
f:x\rightarrow y
in
\mathscr{X}
, the horizontal morphisms in the lax square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k(x)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h(x)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k(y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h(y)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{x}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta_{y}}

admit left adjoints and the associated mate of the square is an equivalence.

Conventions

We will use the language of
\infty
-categories as developed by Joyal and Lurie. Moreover, we use the following notational conventions:

•

The
\infty
-categories of spaces (i.e.
\infty
-groupoids) and
\infty
-categories are denoted by
\mathscr{S}
and
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
respectively.

•

For an
\infty
-category
\mathscr{C}
, we write
\mathrm{PSh}(\mathscr{C}):=\mathrm{Fun}(\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{op}},\mathscr{S})
for the
\infty
-category of presheaves on
\mathscr{C}
.

•

We will use uppercase notation for
(\infty,2)
-categorical upgrades of particular
\infty
-categories. The double
\infty
-categorical variants will be decorated with blackboard bolds.
For instance, in this article, we will see both the
(\infty,2)
-category
\mathrm{CAT}_{\infty}
and the double
\infty
-category
\mathbb{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
of
\infty
-categories.

2.
Preliminaries on exponentiability

This first section is dedicated to reviewing and collecting both the
definitions and basic results concerning
exponentiable objects
and
ideals
in
\infty
-categories, and
(locally) cartesian closed

\infty
-categories.

Definition 2.1
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be an
\infty
-category with finite products. Then an object
x\in\mathscr{C}
is called
exponentiable

if the functor
x\times(-):\mathscr{C}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}
admits a right adjoint. If all objects
in
\mathscr{C}
are exponentiable, then
\mathscr{C}
is called
cartesian closed
.

Notation 2.2
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a cartesian closed
\infty
-category. Then we will denote the internal hom functor by

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{C}}:\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathscr{C}\rightarrow\mathscr{C},

which has the defining property that its composite with the Yoneda embedding

\mathscr{C}\rightarrow\mathrm{PSh}(\mathscr{C})
is given by

\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathscr{C}\rightarrow\mathrm{PSh}(\mathscr{C}):(x,y)\mapsto\mathrm{Map}_{\mathscr{C}}((-)\times x,y).

There is also a local notion of exponentiability. To this end, note that if
\mathscr{C}
is an
\infty
-category with pullbacks then all
its slices admit finite products and these are computed by pullbacks in
\mathscr{C}
.

Proposition 2.3
.

Suppose that
f:x\rightarrow y
is an arrow in an
\infty
-category
\mathscr{C}
with pullbacks. Then the following assertions are equivalent:

(1)

the functor
f^{*}:\mathscr{C}/y\rightarrow\mathscr{C}/x
admits a right adjoint,

(2)

the arrow
f
is exponentiable when viewed as an object of
\mathscr{C}/y
,

(3)

for every arrow
g:a\rightarrow y
, the functor
(f^{*}g)^{*}:\mathscr{C}/a\rightarrow\mathscr{C}/(a\times_{y}x)
admits a right adjoint.

Proof.

The product functor
f\times(-):\mathscr{C}/y\rightarrow\mathscr{C}/y
decomposes as a composite

\mathscr{C}/y\xrightarrow{f^{*}}\mathscr{C}/x\xrightarrow{f_{!}}\mathscr{C}/y,

and this proves that (1) implies (2). It is clear that (3) implies (1).
Hence, it remains to show that (2) implies (3). Suppose that we have an adjunction

f\times(-):\mathscr{C}/y\rightleftarrows\mathscr{C}/y:[f,-]

with unit
\eta
. Let
g:a\rightarrow y
be an arrow.
Passing to slices over
g
gives rise to an adjunction

\mathscr{C}/a\simeq(\mathscr{C}/y)/g\rightleftarrows(\mathscr{C}/y)/(f\times g)\simeq\mathscr{C}/(a\times_{y}x)

where the right adjoint is given by the composite

(\mathscr{C}/y)/(f\times g)\xrightarrow{[f,-]}(\mathscr{C}/y)/[f,f\times g]\xrightarrow{\eta_{g}^{*}}(\mathscr{C}/y)/g,

on account of
[
Lur09a
, Proposition 5.2.5.1]
. The left adjoint is precisely given by base change along

f^{*}g
.
∎

Definition 2.4
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be an
\infty
-category with pullbacks. Then an arrow
f:x\rightarrow y
in
\mathscr{C}

is called
exponentiable
if the equivalent conditions of
2.3
are met.
If every arrow in
\mathscr{C}
is exponentiable, then
\mathscr{C}
is called
locally cartesian closed
.

Example 2.5
.

The exponentiable arrows in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
are also called
Conduché fibrations
. They were studied
by Ayala and Francis in
[
AF20
]
and by Lurie in
[
Lur17
, Section B.3]
.

Example 2.6
.

Since colimits in
\infty
-toposes are universal, all
\infty
-toposes are locally cartesian closed.

We highlight the following implications of
2.3
:

Corollary 2.7
.

The class of exponentiable arrows in an
\infty
-category with pullbacks
is closed under pullbacks.

Corollary 2.8
.

An
\infty
-category
\mathscr{C}
with pullbacks is locally cartesian closed if and only if the
slice
\mathscr{C}/x
is cartesian closed for every
x\in\mathscr{C}
.

It will be helpful later in this article, to recognize subcategories of cartesian closed
\infty
-categories
that are again cartesian closed. To this end, we will use the formalism of
exponential ideals
(cf.
[
Joh02
, Section 1.5]
).

Definition 2.9
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a cartesian closed
\infty
-category.
A fully faithful functor
i:\mathscr{I}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}
is called an
exponential ideal

if for every
x\in\mathscr{C}
and
y\in\mathscr{I}
, the internal hom
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{C}}(x,iy)
is contained in
\mathscr{I}
.

Proposition 2.10
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be a cartesian closed
\infty
-category.
Suppose that
i:\mathscr{I}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}
is an exponential ideal so that its essential image is closed under finite products. Then
\mathscr{I}
is again cartesian closed, and
i

preserves internal homs.

Proof.

Since
i
is fully faithful, it reflects limits. Hence
\mathscr{I}
is closed under finite products and
i
preserves them. It follows that for objects
t,x,y
in
\mathscr{I}
,
we have natural equivalences

\mathrm{Map}_{\mathscr{I}}(t\times x,y)\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\mathscr{C}}(it\times ix,iy)\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\mathscr{C}}(it,\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{C}}(ix,iy)).

Since
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{C}}(ix,iy)
is in the image of
i
, the result follows.
∎

Remark 2.11
.

Note that the full inclusion of a reflective subcategory is always closed under finite products.

The following observation is immediate, and we omit its proof:

Lemma 2.12
.

Suppose that
i:\mathscr{I}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}
is a reflective subcategory of a cartesian closed
\infty
-category
\mathscr{C}
, so that
i

admits a left adjoint
L:\mathscr{C}\rightarrow\mathscr{I}
.
Let
x\in\mathscr{C}
be an object. Then the following assertions are equivalent:

(1)

the functor

x\times(-):\mathscr{C}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}

preserves
L
-local equivalences,

(2)

if
S
is a class of generating
L
-local equivalences, then
x\times f
is a
L
-local equivalence for every
f\in S
,

(3)

the functor
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathscr{C}}(x,-)
carries objects in
\mathscr{I}
to objects in
\mathscr{I}
.

Proposition 2.13
.

A reflective subcategory
i:\mathscr{I}\rightarrow\mathscr{C}
is an exponential ideal if and only if the reflector

L:\mathscr{C}\rightarrow\mathscr{I}
preserves finite products.

Proof.

It readily follows from characterization (1) of
2.12
that
i
is an exponential ideal if
L
preserves finite products.
Conversely, suppose that
i
is an exponential ideal.
Then we have to show that for any two objects
x,y\in\mathscr{C}
, the canonical comparison map

L(x\times y)\rightarrow Lx\times Ly
is an equivalence. Let us denote the unit of the adjunction of
(L,i)
by
\eta
.
Then we obtain a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L(x\times y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L(x\times iLy)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L(iLx\times iLy)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Lx\times Ly}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Lx\times LiLy}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces LiLx\times LiLy.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L(x\times\eta_{y})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L(\eta_{x}\times iLy)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Lx\times L\eta_{y}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L\eta_{x}\times LiLy}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

The rightmost vertical arrow is an equivalence since
L
is a reflector and
i
preserves finite products. By characterization (1) of
2.12
, the two top horizontal arrows are equivalences as well.
Hence, the desired result follows from 2-out-of-3.
∎

3.
Preliminaries on double Segal spaces

In this section, we will review and study a selection of types of Barwick’s
double
and
2-fold Segal spaces

with different completeness conditions. We will then proceed to show that
their ambient
\infty
-categories are cartesian closed by using the theory of exponential ideals
that was discussed in
2
.

3.1.
Double Segal spaces

Double Segal spaces arise as certain presheaves on
\Delta^{\times 2}
. Throughout,
we will write

[n,m]\in\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})

for the image of
([n],[m])
under the Yoneda embedding
\Delta^{\times 2}\rightarrow\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})
.

Definition 3.1
.

The presheaves
[0,0]
,
[1,0]
,
[0,1]
and
[1,1]
are called the
free-living object
(or
0-cell
), the
free-living horizontal
and
vertical arrow
(or
1-cell
), and the
free-living 2-cell
,
respectively.

Definition 3.2
.

A double Segal space is a presheaf on
\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})
that is local with respect to the class of following maps:

(
Seg
)

the spine inclusions

[1,m]\cup_{[0,m]}[1,m]\dotsb\cup_{[0,m]}[1,m]\rightarrow[n,m],

and

[n,1]\cup_{[n,0]}[n,1]\dotsb\cup_{[n,0]}[n,1]\rightarrow[n,m]

for
n,m\geq 0
.

We will write

\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S})\subset\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})

for the reflective full subcategory of double Segal spaces.

Remark 3.3
.

Unraveling the definitions, we observe that a double Segal space
\mathscr{P}
contains:

•

a space
\mathscr{P}_{0,0}
of objects,

•

a space
\mathscr{P}_{0,1}
of
vertical arrows
,

•

a space
\mathscr{P}_{1,0}
of
horizontal arrows
,

•

and a space
\mathscr{P}_{1,1}
of

2-cells
.

In general, the Segal condition for
\mathscr{P}

tells us that we may view
\mathscr{P}_{n,m}
as a grid of compatible cells.
The 2-cells and the compatible arrows of
\mathscr{P}
that have the same direction may be composed in a mutually compatible and coherently associative fashion. A 2-cell may be pictured as a square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}

where the horizontal/vertical directed arrows are horizontal/vertical arrows of
\mathscr{P}
.

Remark 3.4
.

Double Segal spaces are generalizations of Rezk’s Segal spaces
[
Rez01
]
. Recall that the
\infty
-category

\mathrm{Seg}(\mathscr{S})\subset\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta)

of Segal spaces is
defined to be the reflective subcategory
generated by the spine inclusions
[1]\cup_{[0]}[1]\cup_{[0]}\dotsb\cup_{[0]}[1]\rightarrow[n]
for
n\geq 0
.
Here, we implicitly view every
[n]\in\Delta
as an object of
\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta)
via the Yoneda embedding.

Construction 3.5
.

We will use three involutive operations on
\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})
:

(-)^{\mathrm{t}},(-)^{\mathrm{hop}},(-)^{\mathrm{vop}}:\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})\rightarrow\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2}),

called the
transpose
,
horizontal opposite
, and
vertical opposite
respectively. They are defined by restricting along the functors

\displaystyle\mathrm{t}:\Delta\times\Delta\rightarrow\Delta\times\Delta:([n],[m])\mapsto([m],[n]),

\displaystyle\mathrm{hop}:\Delta\times\Delta\xrightarrow{\mathrm{op}\times\mathrm{id}}\Delta\times\Delta,\quad\mathrm{vop}:\Delta\times\Delta\xrightarrow{\mathrm{id}\times\mathrm{op}}\Delta\times\Delta.

Here
\mathrm{op}:\Delta\rightarrow\Delta
denotes the usual opposite involution, i.e. it is restricted
from the involution functor
(-)^{\mathrm{op}}
for categories.

One readily verifies that these involutions restrict to involutions

(-)^{\mathrm{t}},(-)^{\mathrm{hop}},(-)^{\mathrm{vop}}:\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S})\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S}),

on the
\infty
-category
of double Segal spaces.

3.2.
Two-fold Segal spaces

As will be dicussed later in further detail, double Segal spaces may be used
to obtain Barwick’s model of
(\infty,2)
-categories. To this end, we will need
to consider the double Segal spaces with only degenerate vertical arrows.

Definition 3.6
.

A double Segal space
X
is called a
2-fold Segal space
if the structure map

(\mathrm{id},s_{0})^{*}:X_{0,0}\rightarrow X_{0,1}

is an equivalence.
In other words, a presheaf
X\in\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})
is a 2-fold Segal space
if it is local with respect to (
Seg
) of
3.2
, and additionally to:

(
deg
)

the degeneracy map
[0,1]\rightarrow[0,0].

We will write

\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})\subset\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})

for the reflective subcategory of
\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})
spanned by those 2-fold Segal spaces.

Note that every 2-fold Segal space
X\in\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})
has the feature that the restriction
X_{0,\bullet}
is an essentially constant simplicial space.
It will be convenient to consider the subcategory of such presheaves.

Definition 3.7
.

We will write

(-)_{h}:\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})_{\mathrm{deg}}\rightarrow\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})

for the inclusion of the full subcategory of those presheaves
X\in\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})

so that
X_{0,\bullet}
is essentially constant. This is called the

horizontal inclusion
functor. Note that this is a inclusion of a reflective
subcategory, with generating local maps given by the degeneracy maps
[0,n]\rightarrow[0,0]
for all
n
.

Construction 3.8
.

There is also a
vertical inclusion
functor

(-)_{v}:\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})_{\mathrm{deg}}\rightarrow\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})

given by the composite

\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})_{\mathrm{deg}}\xrightarrow{(-)_{h}}\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})\xrightarrow{(-)^{\mathrm{t}}}\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})\xrightarrow{(-)^{\mathrm{hop}}}\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2}).

Note that this restricts to a functor
\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S})
.

Construction 3.9
.

Let
\mathrm{Gaunt}_{2}
be the full subcategory of the category of 2-categories that is spanned by the

gaunt 2-categories
. Then one can write down the bicosimplicial object

\Delta^{\times 2}\rightarrow\mathrm{Gaunt}_{2}:([n],[m])\mapsto[n;m,\dotsc,m],

where
[n;m\dotsc,m]
denotes the globular 2-category that may be pictured as

{[n;m,\dotsc,m]}:=\hbox to578.32pt{\vbox to106.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 291.7777pt\lower-53.60669pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

The restriction to
\{[0]\}\times\Delta
is constant, so that the we obtain an induced functor

N:\mathrm{Gaunt}_{2}\rightarrow\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})_{\mathrm{deg}},

so that the image of a gaunt 2-category
G
is level-wise described by the
set

NG_{n,m}=\mathrm{Hom}([n;m,\dotsc,m],G)

of functors from
[n;m,\dotsc,m]
to
G
.
One readily verifies that the functor
N
is fully faithful; we will leave the inclusion
N
implicit throughout this chapter.

Remark 3.10
.

The obvious commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{0,\dotsc,n\}_{h}\times{[m]_{v}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[n,m]=[n]_{h}\times[m]_{v}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{0,\dotsc,n\}_{h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[n;m,\dotsc,m]}_{h},}

of bisimplicial spaces is a pushout square in
\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})
, as one readily verifies. If
X
is a presheaf in
\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})_{\mathrm{deg}}
,
then
X
is local with respect to the left map in this square. Consequently,
X
is also local to the right (quotient) map in the square.
It follows that the we obtain a natural equivalence

\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})}([n;m\dotsc,m]_{h},X)\rightarrow\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})}([n,m],X)=X_{n,m}.

This observation implies that
\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})_{\mathrm{deg}}
is a presheaf
\infty
-topos. Namely, let
\Theta_{2}^{\prime}\subset\mathrm{Gaunt}_{2}
be the full subcategory spanned by the
2-categories
[n;m,\dotsc,m]
,
n,m\geq 0
. Then we obtain an adjunction

\mathrm{PSh}(\Theta_{2}^{\prime})\rightleftarrows\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})_{\mathrm{deg}}

where the left adjoint is the cocontinuous extension of the restricted nerve
N|\Theta_{2}^{\prime}
,
and the right adjoint is induced by restriction along the bicosimplicial object
\Delta^{\times 2}\rightarrow\Theta_{2}^{\prime}
.
Using the above, one readily concludes that this sets up an adjoint equivalence.

In turn, this can be used to recover the comparison of
[
BR20
]
between 2-fold Segal spaces and
\Theta_{2}
-spaces
. Namely,
the subcategory
\Theta_{2}\subset\mathrm{Gaunt}_{2}
of
Joyal disks

[n;m_{1},\dotsc,m_{n}]
(see
[
Rez10
]
) is generated by
\Theta_{2}^{\prime}
under retracts.
So the inclusion
\Theta_{2}^{\prime}\subset\Theta_{2}
induces an equivalence
\mathrm{PSh}(\Theta_{2})\rightarrow\mathrm{PSh}(\Theta_{2}^{\prime})
. A direct verification
shows that the
composed equivalence

\mathrm{PSh}(\Theta_{2})\rightarrow\mathrm{PSh}(\Theta_{2}^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})_{\mathrm{deg}}

derives to an equivalence between
\Theta_{2}
-spaces and 2-fold Segal spaces.

.

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

Construction 3.12
.

Note that there is an adjunction
\Delta\times\Delta\rightleftarrows\Delta
, where the left adjoint is given by projection onto the first coordinate,
and the right adjoint is given by the inclusion of the subcategory
\Delta\times\{[0]\}
. This gives rise to an adjunction

\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta)\rightleftarrows\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})
between presheaf
\infty
-categories, that in turn restricts to an adjunction

\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta)\rightleftarrows\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})_{\mathrm{deg}}:(-)^{(1)}.

We will leave the fully faithful left adjoint implicit throughout this chapter; every simplicial
space is viewed as a presheaf in
\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})_{\mathrm{deg}}
via this functor.
The above adjunction also restricts to give an adjunction

\mathrm{Seg}(\mathscr{S})\rightleftarrows\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S}):(-)^{(1)}

as well.

We will use the following special names for the vertical and horizontal
opposites of 2-fold Segal spaces:

Definition 3.13
.

If
X
is a 2-fold Segal space, then its
1-opposite
and
2-opposite

are defined by respectively
X^{1-\mathrm{op}}:=X^{\mathrm{hop}}
and
X^{2-\mathrm{op}}
:=
X^{\mathrm{vop}}
.

Moreover, we recall the definition of the associated mapping Segal spaces of a 2-fold Segal space:

Definition 3.14
.

If
x
and
y
are objects of a 2-fold Segal space
X
, then we will write
X(x,y)
for the Segal space
of maps from
x
to
y
that is
defined by the pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X(x,y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{1,\bullet}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{(x,y)\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{0,\bullet}^{\times 2}.}

3.3.
Horizontal and vertical fragments

On account of
[
Hau18
, Proposition 4.12]
, the inclusion
(-)_{h}:\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S})
admits a right adjoint.
We will give an alternative short proof of this here.

Construction 3.15
.

We will write

\mathrm{Hor}(-):\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})\rightarrow\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})_{\mathrm{deg}}.

for the functor that is induced by the bicosimplicial object

\Delta^{\times 2}\rightarrow\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2}):([n],[m])\mapsto[n;m,\dotsc,m]_{h}.

Proposition 3.16
.

The functor
\mathrm{Hor}(-)
is right adjoint to
(-)_{h}
.

Proof.

Let us write

L:\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})\rightarrow\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})_{\mathrm{deg}}
for the left adjoint to
(-)_{h}
.
Recall from
3.10
that we have a quotient map

[n,m]\rightarrow[n;m,\dotsc,m]_{h}

that is natural in
\Delta^{\times 2}
and is carried to an equivalence by
L
.
Let
X
and
Y
be presheaves on
\Delta^{\times 2}
. Then we now obtain natural equivalences

\displaystyle\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})}(X,\mathrm{Hor}(\mathscr{P}))

\displaystyle\simeq{\lim_{[n,m]\in(\Delta^{\times 2}/X)^{\mathrm{op}}}}\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})}([n,m],\mathrm{Hor}(\mathscr{P}))

\displaystyle\simeq{\lim_{[n,m]\in(\Delta^{\times 2}/X)^{\mathrm{op}}}}\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})}([n;m\dotsc,m]_{h},\mathscr{P})

\displaystyle\simeq{\lim_{[n,m]\in(\Delta^{\times 2}/X)^{\mathrm{op}}}}\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})}((L[n,m])_{h},\mathscr{P})

\displaystyle\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})}((LX)_{h},\mathscr{P}).

In the last step, we used that
(-)_{h}
preserves colimits, which follows from the easily verifiable fact that
\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})_{\mathrm{deg}}

is closed under colimits in
\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})
.
∎

Remark 3.17
.

Note that the functor
\mathrm{Hor}(-)
carries double Segal spaces to 2-fold Segal spaces. Consequently, we obtain a restricted adjunction

(-)_{h}:\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})\rightleftarrows\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S}):\mathrm{Hor}(-).

If
\mathscr{P}
is a double Segal space, then
\mathrm{Hor}(\mathscr{P})
is called the
horizontal fragment
of
\mathscr{P}
.
This 2-fold Segal space may be described by the pullback squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hor}(\mathscr{P})_{n,m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}_{n,m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}_{0,0}^{\times(n+1)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}_{0,m}^{\times(n+1)}}

level-wise. This follows from
3.10
; see also
[
Hau18
, Remark 4.13]
.

Definition 3.18
.

It now directly follows that the vertical inclusion
(-)_{v}:\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S})
admits a right adjoint as well.
We will write

\mathrm{Vert}(-):\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S})\rightarrow\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S}):\mathscr{P}\mapsto\mathrm{Hor}((\mathscr{P}^{\mathrm{hop}})^{\mathrm{t}})

for its right adjoint. This is called the
vertical fragment
functor.

Remark 3.19
.

One may show that the functors
\mathrm{Hor}(-)
and
\mathrm{Vert}(-)
are compatible with taking opposites as follows:
there are natural equivalences

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hor}(\mathscr{P}^{\mathrm{hop}})\simeq\mathrm{Hor}(\mathscr{P})^{1-\mathrm{op}},\quad\mathrm{Hor}(\mathscr{P}^{\mathrm{vop}})\simeq\mathrm{Hor}(\mathscr{P})^{2-\mathrm{op}},

\displaystyle\mathrm{Vert}(\mathscr{P}^{\mathrm{hop}})\simeq\mathrm{Vert}(\mathscr{P})^{2-\mathrm{op}},\quad\mathrm{Vert}(\mathscr{P}^{\mathrm{vop}})\simeq\mathrm{Vert}(\mathscr{P})^{1-\mathrm{op}},

for every double Segal space
\mathscr{P}
.

Note that
\mathrm{Vert}(\mathscr{P})\simeq\mathrm{Hor}(\mathscr{P}^{\mathrm{t}})^{2-\mathrm{op}}
and

X_{v}\simeq(X^{2-\mathrm{op}})_{h}^{\mathrm{t}}
.
One could also have defined the vertical inclusion and vertical fragments in such a way that there would not appear 2-opposites in these descriptions.
However, our introduced convention seems more natural regarding the
material that will follow.

3.4.
Completeness conditions

So far, we have not talked about completeness conditions
for 2-fold and double Segal spaces. We will start by recalling the situation for Segal spaces
[
Rez01
]
.

Definition 3.20
.

We define
J\in\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta)
to be the simplicial set defined by the pushout square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1]^{\sqcup 2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[3]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[0]^{\sqcup 2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces J}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{0\leq 2\}\sqcup\{1\leq 3\}}

of simplicial spaces.

Definition 3.21
.

A Segal space
X
is called
complete
if it is local with respect to the map

J\rightarrow[0].

Remark 3.22
.

It is a celebrated result of Joyal and Tierney
[
JT07
]
that complete Segal spaces are a model for
\infty
-categories.
Precisely, the functor

\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}\rightarrow\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta):\mathscr{C}\mapsto([n]\mapsto\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}}([n],\mathscr{C})),

obtained by restricting the Yoneda embedding, is fully faithful, with image given by the complete Segal spaces.

To discuss the two-dimensional completeness conditions, we will need an additional suspended version of
J
.

Notation 3.23
.

We will write
[1;J]
for the object that fits in the pushout square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1;1]}^{\sqcup 2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1;3]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1]}^{\sqcup 2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1;J]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[1;\{0\leq 2\}]\sqcup[1;\{1\leq 3\}]}

in
\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})_{\mathrm{deg}}
. Note that there is a canonical map
[1;J]\rightarrow[1]
.

Notation 3.24
.

We will consider the following labeled families of maps:

(
hc
)

[1]_{h}\times J_{v}\rightarrow[1]_{h}
,

(
vc
)

J_{h}\times[1]_{v}\rightarrow[1]_{v}
,

(
lhc
)

[1;J]_{h}\rightarrow[1]_{h}
,

(
lvc
)

[1;J]_{v}\rightarrow[1]_{v}
.

Remark 3.25
.

In light of
3.10
,
we have a pushout square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{0,1\}_{h}\times{J_{v}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1]_{h}\times J_{v}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{0,1\}_{h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1;J]}_{h}}

in
\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})
. Consequently, the map (
hc
) is contained in the saturated
class generated by
(
lhc
) and the map
J_{v}\rightarrow[0]
. Conversely, the map
J_{v}\rightarrow[0]_{v}
is a retract
of (
hc
), so that (
lhc
) and
J_{v}\rightarrow[0]_{v}
are contained in the saturation
of (
hc
).

The following result appears in greater generality in
[
Hau18
, Section 7]
as well.

Proposition 3.26
.

Let
X
be a 2-fold Segal space. Then the following assertions are equivalent:

(1)

for every two objects
x,y\in X
, the Segal space
X(x,y)
is complete,

(2)

X
is local with respect to the map (hc),

(3)

X
is local with respect to the map (lhc).

Proof.

It follows from
3.25
that (2) and (3) are equivalent, since
J_{v}\rightarrow[0]_{v}
is contained in the saturation of (
Seg
) and (
deg
).
To show that (1) and (2) are equivalent, we use that the map
[1]_{h}\times J_{v}\rightarrow[1]_{h}
is cofibered under
\{0,1\}_{h}
.
So, we obtain a commutative triangle

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})}([1]_{h},X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})}([1]_{h}\times J_{v},X).}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})}(\{0,1\}_{h},X)}

Now, (2) holds if and only if the top arrow is an equivalence. This can be checked fiber-wise, which
precisely recovers condition (1).
∎

Definition 3.27
.

Let
X
be a 2-fold Segal space. Then
X
is called
locally complete
if the equivalent conditions of
3.26

are met. We will write

\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})^{lc}\subset\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})

for the reflective subcategory of locally complete
2-fold Segal spaces.

Proposition 3.28
.

Let
X
be a 2-fold Segal space. Then the following are equivalent:

(1)

X^{(1)}
is a complete Segal space,

(2)

X
is local with respect to (vc).

Proof.

This follows from the dual form of
3.25
and the fact that
(\textit{lvc})
is contained in the
saturation of (
Seg
) and (
deg
).
∎

Definition 3.29
.

We say that a 2-fold Segal space
X
is
complete
if it is locally complete and the equivalent conditions of

3.28
are met.

The reflective subcategory of
\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})
that is spanned by the
complete 2-fold Segal spaces meets the characterization of
[
BSP21
]
of the
\infty
-category of
(\infty,2)
-categories; see
[
BSP21
, Theorem 4.16]
.
We will write

\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,2)}\subset\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})

for this full subcategory, and also refer to complete 2-fold Segal spaces as
(\infty,2)
-categories.
There are concrete comparisons between complete 2-fold Segal spaces and other models for
(\infty,2)
-categories in
[
Lur09b
]
,
[
BR13
]
and
[
BR20
]
.

Remark 3.30
.

Note that the adjunction
\mathrm{Seg}(\mathscr{S})\rightleftarrows\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S}):(-)^{(1)}
restricts to an adjunction

\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}\rightleftarrows\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,2)}:(-)^{(1)}
.

Remark 3.31
.

One easily verifies that the nerve functor that was constructed in
3.9
has image
contained in
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,2)}
, so it defines an inclusion

\mathrm{Gaunt}_{2}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,2)}.

Although we will not use it in this chapter, it is worth mentioning that this functor has been extended to a fully faithful functor defined on the whole of
\mathrm{Cat}_{2}
by Moser
[
Mos20
]
.
Here,
\mathrm{Cat}_{2}
denotes the underlying
\infty
-category (actually, a weak
(3,1)
-category) of the
canonical model structure on 2-categories

[
Lac02
]
.

We will now discuss several completeness conditions for double Segal spaces in terms of completeness of their
vertical and horizontal fragments.

Definition 3.32
.

A double Segal space
\mathscr{P}
is called
locally complete
if
\mathrm{Vert}(\mathscr{P})
and
\mathrm{Hor}(\mathscr{P})
are locally complete, i.e. whenever it local with respect to
(
lhc
) and (
vhc
).
We will write

\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S})^{lc}\subset\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S})

for the full subcategory spanned by the locally complete double Segal spaces.

Remark 3.33
.

Note that locally complete double Segal spaces are closed under horizontal and vertical oppposites, and transposes.

Definition 3.34
.

A double Segal space
\mathscr{P}
is called a double
\infty
-category if it is local with respect to (
hc
),
i.e. if
\mathrm{Vert}(\mathscr{P})^{(1)}
is complete and
\mathrm{Hor}(\mathscr{P})
is locally complete (see
3.25
).
We will write

\mathrm{DblCat}_{\infty}\subset\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S})

for the reflective subcategory spanned
by the double
\infty
-categories.

Remark 3.35
.

We may view double
\infty
-categories as simplicial objects in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
as follows.
Applying
\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}},-)
to the embedding that
was discussed in
3.22
, we will obtain a fully faithful functor

i:\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty})\rightarrow\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})

that carries a simplicial
\infty
-category
\mathscr{P}^{\prime}
to the bisimplicial space

i(\mathscr{P}^{\prime})_{n,m}=\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}}([m],\mathscr{P}^{\prime}_{n}).

One readily verifies that this restricts to an equivalence between:

(1)

the
Segal objects
in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
, i.e. simplicial objects
\mathscr{P}^{\prime}:\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}

so that each functor

\mathscr{P}^{\prime}_{n}\rightarrow\mathscr{P}^{\prime}_{1}\times_{\mathscr{P}^{\prime}_{0}}\dotsb\times_{\mathscr{P}^{\prime}_{0}}\mathscr{P}^{\prime}_{1}

is an equivalence for every
n\geq 0
,

(2)

the double
\infty
-categories.

We will leave this identification implicit, and view every double
\infty
-category as a Segal object in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
.

Remark 3.36
.

The subcategory
\mathrm{DblCat}_{\infty}
is not closed under transposition, as one may readilt check. The
involutions
(-)^{\mathrm{hop}}
and
(-)^{\mathrm{vop}}
do restrict to an endofunctor on
\mathrm{DblCat}_{\infty}
.
Suppose that
\mathscr{P}
is a double
\infty
-category, viewed as a simplicial
\infty
-category. Then the vertical opposite
\mathscr{P}

is described level-wise by

(\mathscr{P}^{\mathrm{vop}})_{n}=\mathscr{P}_{n}^{\mathrm{op}}.

Example 3.37
.

In
[
Rui23
]
, we discuss the double
\infty
-category

\mathbb{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}

which is loosely described as follows:

•

its objects are are
\infty
-categories,

•

its vertical arrows are functors,

•

its horizontal arrows are
profunctors
, i.e. a horizontal arrow
F:\mathscr{C}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}
is given by a functor

\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathscr{C}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}
,

•

its 2-cells are given as follows: a 2-cell

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{D}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}

corresponds to a natural transformation

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{B}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathscr{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}.}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathscr{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\mathrm{op}}\times f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}

More generally, we construct in
[
Rui23
]
a double
\infty
-category

\mathbb{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}(\mathscr{E}),

for every suitable
\infty
-category
\mathscr{E}
, whose objects are
\infty
-categories internal to
\mathscr{E}
. In
[
Hau16
]
,
Haugseng constructs a similarly described double
\infty
-category

\mathbb{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}^{\mathscr{V}}

of
\infty
-categories enriched in a suitably monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathscr{V}
.

In practice, double
\infty
-categories, such as these in
3.37
, are usually more conviently constructed as Segal objects in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
. We will briefly discuss how
one may verify the completeness of double
\infty
-categories when viewed as simplicial
\infty
-categories.

Notation 3.38
.

Let
\mathscr{P}
be a double
\infty
-category, viewed as a Segal object in
\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
. Then we write
\mathscr{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}
for the
\infty
-category
defined by the pullback square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}_{0}\times\mathscr{P}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{P}_{1}\times\mathscr{P}_{1}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\{0\leq 2\}^{*},\{1\leq 3\}^{*})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{0}^{*}\times s_{0}^{*}}

Proposition 3.39
.

Suppose that
\mathscr{P}
is a double Segal space. Then the following assertions are equivalent:

(1)

\mathrm{Vert}(\mathscr{P})
is complete and
\mathrm{Hor}(\mathscr{P})
is locally complete,

(2)

\mathscr{P}
is a double
\infty
-category and the canonical functor
\mathscr{P}_{0}\rightarrow\mathscr{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}
is fully faithful.

Proof.

We must show that a double
\infty
-category
\mathscr{P}
is local with respect to (
vhc
)
if and only if
\mathscr{P}_{0}\rightarrow\mathscr{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}
is fully faithful.
This functor is fully faithful if and only if it is left orthogonal to the inclusion
\{0,1\}\rightarrow[1]
, i.e. if and only if the induced square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}}([1],\mathscr{P}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}}([1],\mathscr{P}_{\mathrm{eq}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}}(\{0,1\},\mathscr{P}_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}}(\{0,1\},\mathscr{P}_{\mathrm{eq}})}

is a pullback square. This can be identified with the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})}([1]_{v}\times\{0\}_{h},\mathscr{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})}([1]_{v}\times J_{h},\mathscr{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})}(\{0,1\}_{v}\times\{0\}_{h},\mathscr{P})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})}(\{0,1\}_{v}\times J_{h},\mathscr{P}).}

In light of the pushout square that appears in
3.25
,
this square is a pullback square if and only if the restriction map

\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})}([1]_{v},\mathscr{P})\rightarrow\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})}([1;J]_{v},\mathscr{P})

induced by (
vhc
) is an equivalence.
∎

Example 3.40
.

In
[
Rui23
]
, we show that
\mathbb{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}(\mathscr{E})
is locally complete
for every suitable
\infty
-category
\mathscr{E}
. In particular,
\mathbb{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
is locally complete.
Throughout this article, we will denote its vertical fragment by

\mathrm{CAT}_{\infty}:=\mathrm{Vert}(\mathbb{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}).

It is our preferred model for the
(\infty,2)
-category of
\infty
-categories.

Proposition 3.41
.

Let
\mathscr{P}
be a double Segal space. Then the following assertions are equivalent:

(1)

\mathrm{Vert}(\mathscr{P})
and
\mathrm{Hor}(\mathscr{P})
are complete,

(2)

\mathscr{P}
is local with respect to (hc) and (vc),

(3)

\mathscr{P}
is a double
\infty
-category and the functor
\mathscr{P}_{0}\rightarrow\mathscr{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}
is an equivalence.

Proof.

It is readily verified that (1) and (2) are equivalent using
3.25
. To show that (2) and (3) are equivalent,
we must show that a double
\infty
-category
\mathscr{P}
is local with respect to (
vc
) if and only if

\mathscr{P}_{0}\rightarrow\mathscr{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}
is an equivalence. But this readily
follows from
3.39
, and
the observation that one recovers the map
\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})}([0]_{h},\mathscr{P})\rightarrow\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})}(J_{h},\mathscr{P})

if one applies
\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}}([0],-)
to the functor
\mathscr{P}_{0}\rightarrow\mathscr{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}
.
∎

Definition 3.42
.

A double Segal space is called
complete
if it meets the equivalent conditions of
3.41
. We will write

\mathrm{DblCat}_{\infty}^{c}\subset\mathrm{DblCat}_{\infty}

for the full and reflective subcategory spanned by the complete double Segal spaces.

Remark 3.43
.

The complete double
\infty
-categories are closed under horizontal and vertical opposites, and transposes.

We conclude this subsection by summarizing the reflective subcategory of

\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})
that were constructed above:

3.5.
Exponentiability of double Segal spaces

We will now show the following:

Proposition 3.44
.

The reflective subcategories listed in
3.4.1
are exponential ideals of
\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})
. In particular,
all these subcategories are cartesian closed and inherit the internal homs from
\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})
.

In order to prove
3.44
, we will use the following result of Rezk:

Lemma 3.45

(Rezk)
.

Suppose that
K=[0]
or
J
and consider the the pushout square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{0,1\}\times[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{0,1\}\times K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1]}\times[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P(K)}

in
\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta)
. Then the canonical map
P(K)\rightarrow[1]\times K
is
is contained in the saturated class
of morphisms generated by the spine inclusions.

Proof.

This is essentially the content of
[
Rez01
, Subsection 12.3]
.
∎

Proof of
3.44
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be one of these full subcategories of
\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})
.
Write
S
for the generating local maps of the localization
\mathscr{C}
, as listed in
the table in
3.1
.
Let
X
be a bisimplicial space. Then
2.12
asserts
that we have to show that the functor

X\times(-):\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})\rightarrow\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})

carries each map
f\in S
to a map in the saturation of
S
. Since the product
preserves colimits in each variable, we may reduce to showing this for
X=[n,m]
.
But one can readily verify that
[n,m]
is a retract of
[1,0]^{\times n}\times[0,1]^{\times m}
.
Thus it suffices to handle the cases that

X=[1,0]
and
X=[0,1]
.

Now, in any of the cases that
f
is of type (
Seg
), the desired result can directly be obtained
from the 1-dimensional case
[
Rez01
, Proposition 10.3]
.

Suppose that
f
is of the form (
hc
). If
X=[1]_{h}
, the result readily follows.
Hence, it remains to show that

[1]_{v}\times([1]_{h}\times J_{v})=[1]_{h}\times([1]_{v}\times J_{v})\rightarrow[1]_{h}\times[1]_{v}=[1]_{v}\times[1]_{h}

sits in the saturation of (
Seg
) and (
hc
).
Let
P(K)\rightarrow[1]\times K
be the map of
3.45
for
K=[0]
and
J
.
It follows from this lemma that the vertical arrows in the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1]_{h}}\times P(J)_{v}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1]_{h}}\times P([0])_{v}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1]_{h}}\times[1]_{v}\times J_{v}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1]_{h}}\times[1]_{v}.}

are contained in the saturation of (
Seg
). The upper map is a pushout along
[1]_{h}\times\{0,1\}_{v}\times J_{v}\rightarrow[1]_{h}\times\{0,1\}_{v}
, so it lies in the saturation of (
hc
). Consequently,
by 2-out-of-3, the bottom map lies in the saturation generated by (
Seg
) and (
hc
), as desired.

Let us now handle the map
f
of type (
lhc
) and the case that

X=[1]_{h}
. One can check that the map

([1]\times[1;J])_{h}\rightarrow([1]\times[1])_{h}

is obtained by taking pushouts of the rows in the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[2;J,0]}_{h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[1;J]_{h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[2;0,J]_{h}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[2]}_{h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[1]_{h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[2]_{h}}.}

The middle vertical map is precisely (
lhc
). Consequently, it suffices to check that the outer
vertical maps are contained in the saturation of (
Seg
) and (
lhc
). But this readily follows
from the observation that the canonical inclusions

[1;J]_{h}\cup_{[0]_{h}}[1]_{h}\rightarrow[2;J,0]_{h},\quad\text{and}\quad[1]_{h}\cup_{[0]_{h}}[1;J]_{h}\rightarrow[2;0,J]_{h}

are contained in the saturation (
Seg
).

Next, we handle the case that
f
is of type (
lhc
) and
X=[1]_{v}
. We have to verify that

[1]_{v}\times[1;J]_{h}\rightarrow[1]_{v}\times[1]_{h}

sits in the saturation of (
lhc
) and (
Seg
).
Let
(-)\boxtimes(-)
denote the pushout-product for arrows in
\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})
.
We can then write

[1]_{v}\times[1;K]_{h}=(\{0,1\}_{h}\rightarrow[1]_{h})\boxtimes(([1]\times K)_{v}\rightarrow[1]_{v}).

for
K=[0]
and
J
.
Using the pushout-product, we define

Q(K):=(\{0,1\}_{h}\rightarrow[1]_{h})\boxtimes(P(K)_{v}\rightarrow[1]_{v}),

and obtain a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q(J)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q([0])}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1]_{v}\times[1;J]_{h}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[1]_{v}\times[1]_{h}}.}

The vertical arrows lie in the saturation of (
Seg
) on account of
3.45
.
The top arrow is a pushout along

(\{0,1\}_{h}\rightarrow[1]_{h})\boxtimes((\{0,1\}\times J)_{v}\rightarrow\{0,1\}_{v})\rightarrow(\{0,1\}_{h}\rightarrow[1]_{h})\boxtimes(\{0,1\}_{v}\rightarrow\{0,1\}_{v})

and this is precisely the map
\{0,1\}_{v}\times[1;J]_{h}\rightarrow\{0,1\}_{v}
, which
is contained in the saturation of (
lhc
). Thus the result follows from 2-out-of-3.

The cases (
vc
) and (
lvc
) formally follow from the above cases, and this finishes the proof.
∎

Definition 3.46
.

The
double Segal space of functors
between a
bisimplicial space
X
and a double Segal space
\mathscr{P}
is defined to be

\mathbb{F}\mathrm{un}(X,\mathscr{P}):=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S})}(X,\mathscr{P})=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})}(X,\mathscr{P}).

Definition 3.47
.

The
vertical
and
horizontal cotensor products
of a 2-fold Segal space
X
with a double Segal space are defined as

[X,\mathscr{P}]:=\mathbb{F}\mathrm{un}(X_{v},\mathscr{P})\quad\text{ and }\quad\{X,\mathscr{P}\}:=\mathbb{F}\mathrm{un}(X_{h},\mathscr{P})

respectively.

Note that
\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})_{\mathrm{deg}}
is
not
an exponential ideal of
\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})
. However,
it is still cartesian closed since it is a presheaf
\infty
-topos (see
3.10
).
The following can be readily deduced from
3.44
:

Corollary 3.48
.

The full subcategories
\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})
,
\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})^{lc}
and
\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,2)}
are exponentiable
ideals of
\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})_{\mathrm{deg}}
.

Notation 3.49
.

The
2-fold Segal space of functors
between 2-fold Segal spaces
X
and
Y
is denoted by

\mathrm{FUN}(X,Y):=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})}(X,Y)=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})_{\mathrm{deg}}}(X,Y).

The following result asserts that the defined cotensor products are compatible with fragments:

Proposition 3.50
.

Let
X
and
\mathscr{P}
be a 2-fold and double Segal space respectively. There are canonical equivalences of 2-fold Segal spaces

\mathrm{Vert}([X,\mathscr{P}])\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{FUN}(X,\mathrm{Vert}(\mathscr{P})),\quad\mathrm{Hor}(\{X,\mathscr{P}\})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{FUN}(X,\mathrm{Hor}(\mathscr{P})).

Proof.

This follows immediately from the fact that the inclusions
(-)_{h}
and
(-)_{v}
preserve products.
∎

Remark 3.51
.

Let
X
be an
\infty
-category and suppose that
\mathscr{P}
is a double
\infty
-category. Then the vertical cotensor
double
\infty
-category

[X,\mathscr{P}]
is given by the composite

\Delta^{\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Fun}(X,-)}\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}

as a simplicial
\infty
-category (see
3.35
).
To see this, we note that
X_{v}
is always a double
\infty
-category and
(X_{v})_{n}=X
for all
n
.
Since the functor
[X,-]:\mathrm{DblCat}_{\infty}\rightarrow\mathrm{DblCat}_{\infty}
is right adjoint to the functor

X_{v}\times(-):\mathrm{DblCat}_{\infty}\rightarrow\mathrm{DblCat}_{\infty},

the observation follows.

Example 3.52
.

Let
X
be an
\infty
-category and
\mathscr{C}
be an
\infty
-category with finite limits. Then
we may apply the vertical cotensor construction to the double
\infty
-category
\mathbb{S}\mathrm{pan}(\mathscr{C})
of spans that is
constructed in
[
Hau18
]
.
One readily deduces using
3.51
that there is a canonical equivalence of double
\infty
-categories

[X,\mathbb{S}\mathrm{pan}(\mathscr{C})]\simeq\mathbb{S}\mathrm{pan}(\mathrm{Fun}(X,\mathscr{C})).

Example 3.53
.

Let
T
be an
\infty
-category.
We may apply the above construction to the double
\infty
-category
\mathbb{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
(see
3.37
)
so that we obtain a (locally complete) double
\infty
-category

[T^{\mathrm{op}},\mathbb{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}]

whose objects are called
T
-indexed
\infty
-categories
. Its vertical fragment is given by the

(\infty,2)
-category of functors
\mathrm{FUN}(T^{\mathrm{op}},\mathrm{CAT}_{\infty})
.
It is shown in
[
Rui23
]
that
\mathbb{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}\simeq\mathbb{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}(\mathscr{S})
,
so there is an equivalence

[T^{\mathrm{op}},\mathbb{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}]\simeq[T^{\mathrm{op}},\mathbb{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}(\mathscr{S})].

See
3.37
for the notation.
The right hand-side can be identified with the double
\infty
-category
\mathbb{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}(\mathrm{PSh}(T))
, as may
be concluded directly from the construction of
\mathbb{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}(-)
in
[
Rui23
]
, so that we obtain an equivalence

[T^{\mathrm{op}},\mathbb{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}]\simeq\mathbb{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}(\mathrm{PSh}(T)).

More generally, one can consider the vertical cotensor product

[\mathscr{X}^{1-\mathrm{op}},\mathbb{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}],

where
\mathscr{X}
is an
(\infty,2)
-category. The objects of this double
\infty
-category are functors
\mathscr{X}^{1-\mathrm{op}}\rightarrow\mathrm{CAT}_{\infty}
, and these are usually
called
2-presheaves
.

3.6.
The squares construction

We will make use of the
squares functor
for 2-fold Segal spaces in this article. In the strict context, this construction goes back to Ehresmann
[
Ehr63
]
.
It has been studied by Grandis and Paré
[
GP04
]
, and recently by Guetta-Moser-Sarazola-Verdugo
[
GMSP23
]
as well.
In the
\infty
-categorical context, it was introduced in the work of Gaitsgory–Rozenblyum on derived algebraic geometry,
where it plays an important role in setting up the
(\infty,2)
-categorical foundations for their treatment of six-functor formalisms. It has also been studied recently
by Abellán
[
Abe23
]
.

To define the squares functor, we make use of the gaunt 2-categories

{}{}}{}{}{{}{}}}}}{{}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.39998pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=2.33746pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 962.76 -32.88 L 405.28 -63.83}{fill:none} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\pgfinnerlinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=1.5375pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#FFFFFF} \lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 962.76 -32.88 L 405.28 -63.83}{fill:none} }\lxSVG@closescope {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}{{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{-0.99846}{-0.05544}{0.05544}{-0.99846}{292.89662pt}{-46.12926pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(-0.99846 -0.05544 0.05544 -0.99846 405.28 -63.83)} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.39998pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray=none,stroke-dashoffset=0.0pt} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap=round} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linejoin=round} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M -1.8 3.55 C -0.92 1.68 1.42 0.07 2.76 0 C 1.42 -0.07 -0.92 -1.68 -1.8 -3.55}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@closescope

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

which
are obtained by taking the oplax Gray tensor product of
[n]
and
[m]
; see
[
Gra74
]
for the original definition.
These 2-categories can be organized into
a bicosimplicial object

[\cdot]\otimes[\cdot]:\Delta^{\times 2}\rightarrow\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})_{\mathrm{deg}}:([n],[m])\mapsto[n]\otimes[m].

We will need the following result:

Proposition 3.54
.

Let
n,m\geq 1
. Then the canonical maps

(i)

[1]\otimes[m]\cup_{[0]\otimes[m]}\dotsb\cup_{[0]\otimes[m]}[1]\otimes[m]\rightarrow[n]\otimes[m],

(ii)

[n]\otimes[1]\cup_{[n]\otimes[0]}\dotsb\cup_{[n]\otimes[0]}[n]\otimes[1]\rightarrow[n]\otimes[m],

are equivalences of 2-fold Segal spaces.

Instead of giving a direct proof of
3.54
here, we will show how it can be leveraged from the literature. We will make use
of the following observation:

Lemma 3.55
.

Suppose that
f:X\rightarrow Y
is a map between 2-fold Segal spaces so that:

(1)

f_{0,0}
is an equivalence of spaces,

(2)

f^{(1)}
is carried to an equivalence by the reflector
\mathrm{Seg}(\mathscr{S})\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{\infty}
,

(3)

f
is carried to an equivalence by the reflector
\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,2)}
.

Then
f
is an equivalence of 2-fold Segal spaces.

Proof.

In light of the Segal condition and assumption (1),
we have to verify that
f_{1,0}
and
f_{1,1}
are equivalences of spaces. Since (2) holds, the map

X_{1,0}\rightarrow Y_{1,0}\times_{Y_{0,0}^{\times 2}}X_{0,0}^{\times 2}

induced by
f_{1,0}
must be an equivalence; see
[
Lur09b
, Theorem 1.2.13]
for instance. Hence
f_{1,0}
is an equivalence.
In light of
[
Hau18
, Remark 7.19]
, assumption (1) implies that

X(x,y)_{1}\rightarrow Y(f(x),f(y))_{1}\times_{Y(f(x),f(y))_{0}^{\times 2}}X(x,y)_{0}^{\times 2}

is an equivalence for all objects
x,y\in X
. Recall
that the map
X(x,y)_{n}\rightarrow Y(f(x),f(y))_{n}
is precisely the comparison map

X_{1,n}\rightarrow Y_{1,n}\times_{Y_{0,n}^{\times 2}}X_{0,n}^{\times 2}

on fibers above
(x,y)\in X_{0,0}^{\times 2}\xrightarrow{\simeq}X_{0,n}^{\times 2}
.
Since
f_{1,0}
is an equivalence, it follows that
X(x,y)_{0}\rightarrow Y(x,y)_{0}
is an equivalence. Thus

X(x,y)_{1}\rightarrow Y(f(x),f(y))_{1}
must be an equivalence.
Since this holds for all
x,y
in
X
, this in turn implies that
f_{1,1}
is an equivalence.
∎

Proof.

We will just treat map (1); the other one is handled similarly. Let us consider the inclusion

i:[1]\otimes[m]\cup_{[0]\otimes[m]}\dotsb\cup_{[0]\otimes[m]}[1]\otimes[m]\rightarrow[n]\otimes[m],

of bisimplicial sets, where the iterated pushout on the left is computed in
\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})
.
Then one readily verifies that
i_{0,0}
is the identity. Moreover, one can readily show that
i^{(1)}
lies in the saturated class
spanned by the 1-dimensional spine inclusions (see
3.4
).
Let us consider the reflectors

L:\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})_{\mathrm{deg}}\rightarrow\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S}),\quad L^{\prime}:\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta)\rightarrow\mathrm{Seg}(\mathscr{S}).

One can verify that the (colimit-preserving) functor
(-)^{(1)}:\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})_{\mathrm{deg}}\rightarrow\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta)

carries maps in (
Seg
) to the saturated class of 1-dimensional spine inclusions. So it follows that
L^{\prime}(Li)^{(1)}\simeq L^{\prime}i^{(1)}
is an equivalence.
A similar argument shows that
(Li)_{0,0}
is an equivalence, as every map in (
Seg
) is carried to an equivalence by the evaluation functor

(-)_{0,0}
.

Now,
3.55
asserts that it is sufficient to check that
Li
is carried to an equivalence
by the reflector
\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,2)}
.
This follows from the work of Gagna-Harpaz-Lanari
[
GHL21
]
and
[
HHLN21
, Proposition 5.1.9]
.
∎

Construction 3.56
.

Since
\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})
is cocomplete, the bicosimplicial object

[\cdot]\otimes[\cdot]:\Delta^{\times 2}\rightarrow\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S}):([n],[m])\rightarrow[n]\otimes[m]

extends to a colimit preserving functor
\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})\rightarrow\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})
. On account of
3.54
,
there exists a unique dotted extension in the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S}).}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Gr}}

The horizontal functor admits a right adjoint, which restricts to a right adjoint

\mathrm{Sq}:\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S})

for
\mathrm{Gr}
. This is called the
squares functor
. For a 2-fold Segal space
X
, it is level-wise given by

\mathrm{Sq}(X)_{n,m}:=\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})}([n]\otimes[m],X).

Construction 3.57
.

There is a canonical functor

\iota_{h}^{n,m}:[n]\otimes[m]\rightarrow[n;m,\dotsc,m]

between gaunt 2-categories that is natural in
([n],[m])\in\Delta^{\times 2}
.
The functor
\iota_{h}^{n,m}
selects the non-degenerate oplax
n\times m
grid in
[n;m,\dotsc,m]
where all the vertical arrows in the grid are identities,
pictured as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dotsb}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n-1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dotsb}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n-1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n}

.

.

.

.

.

.

.

.

.

.

.

.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dotsb}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n-1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dotsb}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n-1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

There is also a similarly defined functor

\iota_{v}^{n,m}:[n]^{\mathrm{op}}\otimes[m]\rightarrow[m;n\dotsc,n]

between gaunt 2-categories.
The functor
\iota_{v}^{n,m}
selects the non-degenerate oplax
n\times m
grid in
[m;n\dotsc,n]
where all horizontal arrows in the grid are identities,
pictured as

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dotsb}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dotsb}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}

.

.

.

.

.

.

.

.

.

.

.

.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m-1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m-1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dotsb}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m-1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m-1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dotsb}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces n}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

Let
X
be a 2-fold Segal space. Then
3.10
implies that
X_{h}
and
X_{v}
are (naturally) described by

\displaystyle(X_{h})_{n,m}\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})}([n;m\dotsc,m]_{h},X_{h}),

\displaystyle(X_{v}^{\mathrm{hop}})_{n,m}\simeq\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})}([m;n\dotsc,n]_{v},X_{v}).

In light of these descriptions, we can construct maps

X_{h}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sq}(X),\quad X_{v}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sq}(X)

using the natural transformations
\iota_{h}
and
\iota_{v}
.

Proposition 3.58
.

Let
X
be a 2-fold Segal space.
The functors
X_{h}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sq}(X)
and
X_{v}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sq}(X)
are adjunct to equivalences

X\rightarrow\mathrm{Hor}(\mathrm{Sq}(X))
and
X\rightarrow\mathrm{Vert}(\mathrm{Sq}(X))
.

Proof.

We will just show the statement for the horizontal case; the other case is handled similarly. We have to show that the map

\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})}([n;m\dotsc,m],X)\rightarrow\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})}([n;m,\dotsc,m],\mathrm{Hor}(\mathrm{Sq}(X)))

is an equivalence for all
n,m\geq 0
. Note that this map can be identified with the map

\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S})}([n;m\dotsc,m]_{h},X_{h})\rightarrow\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S})}([n;m,\dotsc,m]_{h},\mathrm{Sq}(X))

induced by
X_{h}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sq}(X)
. In light of
3.10
, this is an equivalence if and only if the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S})}([n;m\dotsc,m]_{h},X_{h})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S})}([n,m],\mathrm{Sq}(X))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S})}([0,0],\mathrm{Sq}(X))^{\times(n+1)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S})}([0,m],\mathrm{Sq}(X))^{\times(n+1)}}

is a pullback square. By construction of the map
X_{h}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sq}(X)
, this square
is obtained from the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{0,\dotsc,n\}\otimes[m]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[n]}\otimes[m]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{0,\dotsc,n\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[n;m,\dotsc,m]}}

of gaunt 2-categories by applying
\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})}(-,X)
. But this square is a pushout square in
\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})
,
as argued in
[
HHLN21
, Lemma 5.1.11]
. Namely, to show that it is a pushout, one may reduce to
n,m\in\{0,1\}
using the co-Segal condition. The only left-over non-trivial case
is
n=m=1
, where it follows from the fact that the canonical map
[1;1]\cup_{[1]^{\sqcup 2}}[2]\rightarrow[1]\otimes[1]
is an equivalence — already in
\mathrm{PSh}(\Delta^{\times 2})_{\mathrm{deg}}
.
∎

Corollary 3.59
.

The squares functor restricts to right adjoints

\mathrm{Sq}:\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})^{lc}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S})^{lc}
and
\mathrm{Sq}:\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,2)}\rightarrow\mathrm{DblCat}_{\infty}^{c}
as well.

Proof.

It readily follows from
3.58
that the squares functor restricts as such.
The left adjoints are then given by the composites

\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})^{lc}\rightarrow\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Gr}}\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S})\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S})^{lc},

\mathrm{Cat}_{(\infty,2)}\rightarrow\mathrm{Seg}^{2}(\mathscr{S})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Gr}}\mathrm{Cat}^{2}(\mathscr{S})\rightarrow\mathrm{DblCat}_{\infty}^{c},

where the functors that appear first and last in the composites are inclusions and localizations respectively.
∎

4.
Companionships and conjunctions, and homotopy coherence

Let us start by defining companionships and conjunctions in a double Segal space.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
f:x\rightarrow y
be a vertical arrow in a double Segal space
\mathscr{P}
. A horizontal arrow

in
\mathscr{P}
is called the

of

In this case,
(f,F)
is called a
companionship
,
\eta
is called the
companionship unit
, and
\epsilon
is called the

companionship counit
.

Dually, a horizontal arrow
F^{\prime}:y\rightarrow x
is called the
conjoint
of
f

when there exist two 2-cells in
\mathscr{P}

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

In this case,
(f,F^{\prime})
is called a
conjunction
,
\eta^{\prime}
is called the
conjunction unit
, and
\epsilon^{\prime}
is called the

conjunction counit
.

Remark 4.2
.

These notions were also considered by Gaitsgory and Rozenblyum in
[
GR17
, Subsection 10.5.1]
, albeit using different terminology.

Remark 4.3
.

Suppose that
f:x\rightarrow y
and
F:y\rightarrow x
are a vertical and horizontal arrow
of a double Segal space
\mathscr{P}
respectively.
Then one may easily deduce that the following assertions are equivalent:

•

the pair
(f,F)
forms a conjunction in
\mathscr{P}
,

•

the pair
(f^{\mathrm{op}},F)
forms a companionship in
\mathscr{P}^{\mathrm{vop}}
,

•

the pair
(f,F^{\mathrm{op}})
forms a companionship in
\mathscr{P}^{\mathrm{hop}}
.

Consequently, the theory of companionships is formally dual to the theory of conjunctions.

Remark 4.4
.

Suppose that
f:x\rightarrow y
and
g:y\rightarrow z
are vertical arrows of a double Segal space
\mathscr{P}
, with
companions
F
and
G
respectively.
Then it is instructive to verify
gf
admits a companion as well, which is given by the composite
GF
. Dually,
if
F^{\prime}
and
G^{\prime}
are conjoints for
f
and
g
respectively, then
gf
has a conjoint given by
F^{\prime}G^{\prime}
.

Example 4.5
.

We consider the double Segal space
\mathrm{Sq}(X)
of squares in a 2-fold Segal space
X

that was defined in
3.6
.
Let
f
be an arrow of
X
, which we will view as a vertical arrow of
\mathrm{Sq}(X)
. Then one easily checks that the companion of
f
in
\mathrm{Sq}(X)
is given by
f
, now viewed as a horizontal arrow.
It is also instructive to verify that the conjoint
of
f
in
\mathrm{Sq}(X)
exists if and only if
f
admits a right adjoint
g
, in which case
the conjoint of
f
is given by
g
. In that case, the conjunction unit and counit for
(f,g)
corresponds to the adjunction unit and counit for
(f,g)
.

Example 4.6
.

Let
\mathscr{C}
be an
\infty
-category with finite limits.
Then we may consider the double
\infty
-category
\mathbb{S}\mathrm{pan}(\mathscr{C})
of spans in
\mathscr{C}
that was constructed in
[
Hau18
]
.
If
f:x\rightarrow y
is an arrow of
\mathscr{C}

that is viewed as a vertical arrow of
\mathbb{S}\mathrm{pan}(\mathscr{C})
, then it has both a companion and conjoint given by the spans

(\mathrm{id}_{x},f):x\rightarrow x\times y,\quad(f,\mathrm{id}_{x}):x\rightarrow y\times x.

Example 4.7
.

In the double
\infty
-category
\mathbb{C}\mathrm{at}_{\infty}
of
\infty
-categories that was informally described in

3.37
, the companion and conjoint of a functor
f:\mathscr{C}\rightarrow\mathscr{D}
are given by the profunctors

\mathscr{D}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathscr{C}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}:(d,c)\mapsto\mathrm{Map}_{\mathscr{D}}(d,f(c)),\quad\mathscr{C}^{\mathrm{op}}\times\mathscr{D}\rightarrow\mathscr{S}:(c,d)\mapsto\mathrm{Map}_{\mathscr{D}}(f(c),d)

respectively. This is shown in
[
Rui23
]
.

As
4.5
already suggests, the notion of adjunctions, companionships and conjunctions are closely related. The following observation attests to this as well.

Proposition 4.8
.

Let
f:x\rightarrow y
be a vertical arrow in a double Segal space
\mathscr{P}
with a companion
F:x\rightarrow y
and a conjoint

G:y\rightarrow x
.
Then the pair
(F,G)
forms an adjunction in the horizontal fragment
\mathrm{Hor}(\mathscr{P})
.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to the proof of the strict case
[
Shu08
, Proposition 5.3]
.
The candidate unit
\eta
for the adjunction is defined by the pasting

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}

It now readily follows from
the triangle identities for companionships and conjunctions that
\eta
and
\epsilon
satisfy the triangle identities for adjunctions.
∎

4.1.
Homotopy coherence

We will now show that every companionship uniquely upgrades to a homotopy coherent one.
To make this statement concrete, we will need to set up some notation.

Definition 4.9
.

The
free-living companionship
is the (discrete) double Segal space defined by

\mathfrak{comp}:=\mathrm{Sq}([1]).

The dual
free-living conjunction
is given by

\mathfrak{conj}:=\mathfrak{comp}^{\mathrm{hop}}.

Definition 4.10
.

The
free-living lower triangle

is the double Segal space that can be pictured as

L=\hbox to62pt{\vbox to36.53pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 30.99995pt\lower-18.26385pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
